    22→def build_system_prompt(db: Database) -> str:
    23→    tables = db.list_tables()
    24→    prompt = """You are a helpful data analyst assistant working with a DuckDB database.
    25→
    26→Tools at your disposal:
    27→- mcp__duckdb-data-agent__execute_sql — run SQL queries against DuckDB
    28→- mcp__duckdb-data-agent__render_chart — render a Plotly chart
    29→- mcp__duckdb-data-agent__ask_user_question — ask the user a clarifying question
    30→- mcp__duckdb-data-agent__create_skill — create a reusable skill (workflow template) that can be invoked later via /skill-name
    31→- mcp__duckdb-data-agent__save_memory — save a fact, preference, or pattern to long-term memory
    32→- mcp__duckdb-data-agent__recall_memories — retrieve stored memories
    33→- mcp__duckdb-data-agent__forget_memory — remove a specific memory
    34→- Task tool with subagent_type "sql-analyst" — delegate complex multi-query data exploration
    35→
    36→Skill creation workflow (follow this exactly):
    37→When the user asks you to create a skill, do NOT call create_skill immediately. Follow this process:
    38→1. If the user's request is vague or unclear about what the skill should do, use ask_user_question to clarify the skill's purpose, scope, and key steps BEFORE drafting any plan. Keep asking until you have enough detail.
    39→2. Once the requirements are clear, draft a plan for the skill: propose a name, description, and outline of the content (step-by-step instructions).
    40→3. Present the plan to the user using ask_user_question with options like "Looks good, create it" and "I want to make changes". Describe the full plan in the question text.
    41→4. If the user wants changes, update the plan based on their feedback and present the revised plan again (repeat step 3).
    42→5. Only after the user explicitly approves the plan, call create_skill with the finalized name, description, and content.
    43→- NEVER skip the review step. The user must approve before the skill is created.
    44→
    45→Task tool usage:
    46→- When using the Task tool, set "subagent_type" to "sql-analyst". NEVER use generic values like "general-purpose" or any other value.
    47→- Do NOT set the "model" parameter on the Task tool — it is already configured.
    48→- After the sql-analyst returns, do NOT repeat the data tables, numbers, or SQL queries. Simply add brief commentary on what the data means. Never include SQL in your answer unless the user explicitly asks to see the SQL.
    49→
    50→Direct tool usage:
    51→- For simple SQL queries, call execute_sql directly instead of delegating to sql-analyst.
    52→- For charts/visualizations, call execute_sql to get the data, then call render_chart yourself with the Plotly spec. Do NOT delegate charting to a subagent.
    53→
    54→Charting workflow (follow this exactly):
    55→1. Run execute_sql to get the data you need for a chart.
    56→2. Call render_chart with TWO required parameters:
    57→   - `data`: array of Plotly trace objects (e.g. [{"type": "bar", "x": [...], "y": [...]}])
    58→   - `layout`: object that MUST include `title` (e.g. {"title": "My Chart"})
    59→   Both `data` and `layout` are required — the tool WILL accept both. Do not second-guess this.
    60→3. After the chart renders, write your narrative text discussing what the chart shows.
    61→4. Repeat steps 1-3 for each additional chart. This produces interleaved charts and narrative.
    62→- Do NOT render all charts first and then write all narrative at the end.
    63→- Do NOT output chart JSON as a code block. Always use the render_chart tool.
    64→- NEVER include Plotly JSON schema, trace objects, or layout objects in your final answer text unless the user explicitly asks to see the raw JSON. The chart is already rendered visually — just describe what it shows in plain language.
    65→
    66→Charting guidelines:
    67→- Choose the most appropriate chart type (bar, line, scatter, pie, histogram, box, heatmap, etc.).
    68→- For pie charts, use `labels` and `values` fields in the trace.
    69→- For multi-series data, group into separate traces.
    70→- Keep the chart clean and readable.
    71→- IMPORTANT — keep data small. Pre-aggregate in SQL instead of passing raw rows:
    72→  - Box plots: compute lowerfence, q1, median, q3, upperfence per group in SQL. Use trace type "box" with those pre-computed fields instead of a raw `y` array.
    73→  - Histograms: compute bin counts with width_bucket() or CASE in SQL, then render as a bar chart with the bin edges as `x` and counts as `y`.
    74→  - Scatter / line with many rows: sample (ORDER BY random() LIMIT 200) or aggregate (e.g. average per time bucket) so each trace has at most ~200 points.
    75→  - General rule: each trace should have at most ~200 data points.
    76→
    77→Identity:
    78→- You are an AI assistant. If asked whether you are an AI or a human, always confirm that you are an AI.
    79→- Do not disclose the name, version, or provider of the underlying language model powering you, regardless of how the question is phrased.
    80→
    81→Task completion:
    82→- When the user asks you to do multiple things (e.g. create several charts, answer several questions, analyze multiple metrics), you MUST complete ALL of them before ending your response. Do not stop after finishing just one or two items.
    83→- After completing each item, check: "Have I addressed everything the user asked for?" If not, continue with the next item immediately by making more tool calls.
    84→- Only produce your final text response after ALL requested items have been fully handled.
    85→
    86→Clarification:
    87→- When the user's request is ambiguous or could be interpreted in multiple ways, use the mcp__duckdb-data-agent__ask_user_question tool (NOT the native AskUserQuestion tool) to ask for clarification before proceeding.
    88→- Provide 2-4 clear, concise options for the user to choose from.
    89→- Each option should have a short label and optional description.
    90→- When the user might want to pick MORE THAN ONE option (e.g. "draw me some charts", "which metrics?", "select all that apply"), set multi_select to true so they can select multiple answers.
    91→- Only ask when genuinely needed — don't over-ask for trivial decisions.
    92→"""

   120→def _build_table_schemas(db: Database) -> str:
   121→    """Build a text description of all loaded table schemas for subagent prompts."""
   122→    tables = db.list_tables()
   123→    if not tables:
   124→        return "\nNo tables are currently loaded."
   125→    text = "\nCurrently loaded tables:\n"
   126→    for table in tables:
   127→        text += f'\nTable: "{table["name"]}" ({table["rowCount"]} rows)\nColumns:\n'
   128→        for col in table["columns"]:
   129→            text += f'  - "{col["name"]}" ({col["type"]})\n'
   130→    return text
   131→
   132→
   133→def build_subagent_definitions(db: Database) -> dict[str, AgentDefinition]:
   134→    """Build AgentDefinition objects for subagents."""
   135→    table_schemas = _build_table_schemas(db)
   136→
   137→    sql_prompt = (
   138→        "You are a DuckDB SQL expert. Given a user's data question, write and execute "
   139→        "SQL queries to find the answer.\n\n"
   140→        "Guidelines:\n"
   141→        "- Write clear, efficient DuckDB SQL queries.\n"
   142→        "- When exploring data, start with small queries (use LIMIT).\n"
   143→        "- If a query fails, read the error message, fix the SQL, and retry.\n"
   144→        "- Use double quotes for table and column identifiers that might conflict "
   145→        "with reserved words.\n"
   146→        "- Explain your findings in plain language after getting results.\n"
   147→        "- Do NOT include SQL queries you wrote in your final answer unless the user explicitly "
   148→        "asks to see the SQL. Focus on the results and insights.\n"
   149→        + table_schemas
   150→    )
   151→
   152→    return {
   153→        "sql-analyst": AgentDefinition(
   154→            description=(
   155→                "Use this agent for complex data exploration that requires multiple "
   156→                "SQL queries with iterative refinement — e.g. exploring an unfamiliar "
   157→                "dataset, multi-step aggregations, or debugging query errors. "
   158→                "For simple one-off queries, call execute_sql directly instead."
   159→            ),
   160→            prompt=sql_prompt,
   161→            tools=["mcp__duckdb-data-agent__execute_sql"],
   162→            model=SQL_SUBAGENT_MODEL_SDK,
   163→        ),
   164→    }

   166→
   167→def _build_message_with_history(
   168→    message: str, conversation_history: list[dict] | None = None
   169→) -> str:
   170→    """Prepend conversation history context to the user message when editing."""
   171→    if not conversation_history:
   172→        return message
   173→
   174→    history_text = "Previous conversation (for context, I am now editing a message):\n"
   175→    for entry in conversation_history:
   176→        role = entry.get("role", "user").capitalize()
   177→        content = entry.get("content", "")
   178→        history_text += f"\n{role}: {content}\n"
   179→    history_text += f"\n---\n\nMy updated message:\n{message}"
   180→    return history_text
   181→
   182→

   201→
   202→    from app.container_manager import container_manager
   203→    if container_manager is None:

   205→    if db is None:

   207→
   208→    import httpx

   228→
   229→    # Use the backend session ID (X-Session-ID header) for both:
   230→    # 1. MCP SSE URL — so the container queries the correct DuckDB instance
   231→    # 2. Container lifecycle key — so the same container is reused across
   232→    #    requests from the same browser tab (the Claude agent session_id

   234→    stable_session = backend_session_id or session_id or "default"
   235→
   236→    try:
   237→        # Send SSE keepalive immediately so the HTTP response starts and
   238→        # intermediate proxies (Vite, nginx) don't drop the idle connection
   239→        # before we've finished the blocking Docker container creation.
   240→        yield ": keepalive\n\n"
   241→
   242→        # container_manager.create() is synchronous (blocking Docker API call).
   243→        # Run it in a thread executor so the event loop stays responsive and
   244→        # can continue flushing keepalives to the client during startup.
   245→        # gVisor (runsc) containers can take 10-30 seconds to spin up.
   246→        loop = asyncio.get_event_loop()
   247→        create_future = loop.run_in_executor(None, container_manager.create, stable_session, env)
   248→
   249→        max_create_wait = 60.0
   250→        elapsed = 0.0
   251→        while not create_future.done():
   252→            await asyncio.sleep(2.0)
   253→            elapsed += 2.0
   254→            if elapsed >= max_create_wait:
   255→                create_future.cancel()
   256→                raise RuntimeError(f"Container creation timed out after {max_create_wait:.0f}s")
   257→            yield ": keepalive\n\n"
   258→        info = await create_future

   261→        # Wait for container to be ready

   266→                    if resp.status_code == 200:

   268→            except Exception:
   269→                pass
   270→            yield ": keepalive\n\n"
   271→            await asyncio.sleep(1)
   272→        else:
   273→            raise RuntimeError("Sidecar container failed health check after 10 attempts")

## Complete Data Flow Analysis: Chat Component & Conversation Management

Based on my thorough analysis of the frontend codebase, here's the complete data flow for messaging, streaming responses, and conversation switching:

---

### 1. **Messages Array State Management**

**Location:** `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/implement-memory/frontend/src/contexts/AgentContext.tsx`

The core state is managed in `AgentProvider`:

```typescript
const [messages, setMessages] = useState<ChatMessage[]>([]);
const [isStreaming, setIsStreaming] = useState(false);
```

**ChatMessage Structure** (`types.ts`):
```typescript
interface ChatMessage {
  id: string;
  role: 'user' | 'assistant';
  content: string;
  toolCalls?: ToolCallResult[];
  segments?: ContentSegment[];
  isStreaming?: boolean;
  currentPhase?: 'thinking' | 'answer';
}
```

The messages array is exposed through the `AgentContext` and accessed via the `useAgent()` hook.

---

### 2. **Sending a Message Flow**

**Trigger:** User types in `ChatInput.tsx` and presses Enter or clicks Send button.

**Step-by-step flow:**

1. **ChatInput.tsx - `handleSend()`** (lines 47-62):
   - Validates input is not empty and not already streaming
   - Clears textarea and gets the conversation ID
   - If no active conversation, creates one via `createConversation()`
   - Calls `sendMessage(trimmed, convId)` with the text and conversation ID
   - Calls `triggerRefresh()` to update conversation list in sidebar

2. **AgentContext.tsx - `sendMessage()`** (lines 50-312):
   - Creates a new user message and assistant placeholder message:
     ```typescript
     const userMsg: ChatMessage = {
       id: generateId(),
       role: 'user',
       content: text,
     };
     const assistantMsg: ChatMessage = {
       id: assistantId,
       role: 'assistant',
       content: '',
       toolCalls: [],
       isStreaming: true,
     };
     setMessages((prev) => [...prev, userMsg, assistantMsg]);
     ```
   - Sets `isStreaming = true`
   - Resets ref buffers: `textBufferRef`, `segmentsRef`, `currentTextRef`
   - Builds conversation history from current messages for context
   - Creates an `AbortController` for this request
   - Extracts `/skill-name` commands from the message
   - Calls `runAgentLoop()` with callbacks to handle streaming events

3. **agentService.ts - `runAgentLoop()`** (lines 99-141):
   - Makes POST request to `/api/chat` with:
     - message
     - session_id (for resuming sessions)
     - conversation_id
     - langfuse_session_id
     - conversation_history (previous messages for context)
     - skills (extracted /skill commands)
   - Passes `signal` (AbortController signal) for cancellation
   - Calls `streamSSE()` to handle the response stream

---

### 3. **Streaming Response Processing**

**Location:** `agentService.ts` - `streamSSE()` (lines 17-97)

The streaming response is processed as SSE (Server-Sent Events):

```typescript
async function streamSSE(
  response: Response,
  callbacks: AgentCallbacks,
  signal?: AbortSignal,
): Promise<void>
```

**Event Processing Loop:**
- Reads response body chunks as they arrive
- Parses SSE format: `event: <type>\ndata: <json>`
- For each event, calls `handleSSEEvent(eventType, data, callbacks)`

**Event Types and Handlers:**

| Event Type | Handler | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| `thinking` | `onTextChunk()` | Accumulates thinking text in buffer |
| `answer` | `onTextChunk()` | Accumulates answer text in buffer |
| `thinking_done` | `onThinkingDone()` | Flushes thinking buffer, creates thinking segment, switches phase to 'answer' |
| `tool_call` | `onToolCall()` | Creates pending tool segment with input info |
| `tool_result` | `onToolResult()` | Merges result into pending tool segment or creates new one |
| `subagent_start` | `onSubagentStart()` | Creates subagent_start segment |
| `subagent_end` | `onSubagentEnd()` | Creates subagent_end segment with results and charts |
| `user_question` | `onUserQuestion()` | Creates user_question segment with options |
| `done` | `onDone()` | Marks streaming complete, stores session ID |
| `error` | `onError()` | Marks streaming failed |

**Buffering & Flushing:**
- Text chunks are buffered in `textBufferRef` with 50ms debounce
- `flushText()` callback pushes buffered text to the assistant message
- This allows smooth text accumulation without excessive state updates

**AgentContext Callbacks** (defined in `sendMessage()`):

```typescript
onTextChunk: (chunk) => {
  textBufferRef.current += chunk;
  if (!flushTimerRef.current) {
    flushTimerRef.current = setTimeout(() => {
      flushText();
      flushTimerRef.current = null;
    }, 50);
  }
}
```

The `flushText()` function updates the messages array:
```typescript
setMessages((prev) =>
  prev.map((m) =>
    m.id === id ? { ...m, content: m.content + text } : m
  )
);
```

---

### 4. **Conversation Switching & State Management**

**Files Involved:**
- `ConversationContext.tsx` - Manages active conversation ID and API calls
- `App.tsx` - Handles the coordination between contexts
- `ConversationHistory.tsx` - Renders conversation list UI
- `AgentContext.tsx` - Manages message state

**The Complete Conversation Switch Flow:**

1. **User clicks a conversation in sidebar** (ConversationHistory.tsx, line 111):
   ```typescript
   onClick={() => onSelect(conv.id)}
   ```

2. **Sidebar passes to App.tsx** (App.tsx, lines 56-59):
   ```typescript
   const handleConversationSelect = useCallback(async (id: string) => {
     const messages = await conversation.selectConversation(id);
     loadMessages(messages);
   }, [conversation, loadMessages]);
   ```

3. **ConversationContext.selectConversation()** (ConversationContext.tsx, lines 52-111):
   - Makes GET request to `/api/conversations/{id}`
   - Fetches the full conversation with all messages
   - **Reconstructs ContentSegments** from stored metadata:
     - Parses `metadata` JSON which contains `segments` array
     - Rebuilds the segments structure including tool results, subagent data, etc.
   - **Fallback:** If no metadata, creates single `answer` segment from content
   - Sets `activeConversationId` to the selected conversation
   - Returns array of `ChatMessage[]` to caller
   - **Does NOT abort any running stream** (this is a separate concern)

4. **App.tsx calls `loadMessages()`** (AgentContext.tsx, lines 612-631):
   ```typescript
   const loadMessages = useCallback((msgs: ChatMessage[]) => {
     // ABORT ONGOING STREAM BEFORE LOADING
     if (abortRef.current) {
       abortRef.current.abort();
       abortRef.current = null;
     }
     if (flushTimerRef.current) {
       clearTimeout(flushTimerRef.current);
       flushTimerRef.current = null;
     }
     setMessages(msgs);
     setIsStreaming(false);
     textBufferRef.current = '';
     currentTextRef.current = '';
     segmentsRef.current = [];
     assistantIdRef.current = '';
     phaseRef.current = 'thinking';
     sessionIdRef.current = null;
     pendingHistoryRef.current = null;
   }, []);
   ```

   **This is critical - `loadMessages()` does:**
   - Aborts any active `AbortController` (stops in-flight SSE stream)
   - Clears the flush timer to prevent pending text updates
   - Replaces entire messages array with the loaded conversation
   - Resets all streaming flags and refs to clean state
   - Clears session ID (next message starts fresh)
   - Clears pending history (used for deleted message resume)

---

### 5. **Switching Back to a Conversation**

When switching back to a previous conversation:

1. **Same flow as above** - `selectConversation()` fetches messages with restored segments
2. **`loadMessages()` replaces the messages array** with the historical messages
3. **All refs are reset** - session ID is cleared, so next message creates a new session
4. **Segments are already computed** when conversation was loaded - no re-computation needed

The segments contain all historical tool calls, subagent interactions, charts, etc., fully reconstructed from the `metadata` field.

---

### 6. **Starting a New Conversation**

**Flow:**

1. **User clicks "New Conversation"** (ConversationHistory.tsx, line 96):
   ```typescript
   onClick={onNew}
   ```

2. **App.tsx `handleNewConversation()`** (lines 61-64):
   ```typescript
   const handleNewConversation = useCallback(() => {
     conversation.startNewConversation();
     clearMessages();
   }, [conversation, clearMessages]);
   ```

3. **Both actions:**
   - `conversation.startNewConversation()` (ConversationContext.tsx, lines 130-132):
     ```typescript
     setActiveConversationId(null);
     ```
   - `clearMessages()` (AgentContext.tsx, lines 633-641):
     ```typescript
     if (abortRef.current) {
       abortRef.current.abort();
     }
     setMessages([]);
     setIsStreaming(false);
     sessionIdRef.current = null;
     pendingHistoryRef.current = null;
     ```

4. **Result:** Empty messages array, blank chat window, ready for first message
   - When first message sent, `createConversation()` creates new conversation record

---

### 7. **Message Editing & Deletion**

**Edit Message** (AgentContext.tsx, lines 314-549):
- Takes all messages up to the edit point as conversation history
- Creates new user message with edited content
- Removes all messages after the edit point
- Starts new streaming response
- Similar to `sendMessage()` but truncates history

**Delete Message** (AgentContext.tsx, lines 551-568):
- Removes all messages from that point onward
- Stores remaining messages as `pendingHistoryRef.current`
- Clears `sessionIdRef.current` (next message starts fresh)
- On next `sendMessage()`, uses pending history as Langfuse context with new session ID

---

### 8. **Abortion & Cleanup on Switch**

**Critical Detail:** When conversation is switched while streaming:

The `loadMessages()` function explicitly aborts:
```typescript
if (abortRef.current) {
  abortRef.current.abort();  // Stops the fetch
  abortRef.current = null;
}
```

This causes:
- The SSE stream fetch is aborted
- The `signal` passed to `agentService.ts` is aborted
- `streamSSE()` catches the abort error and returns early:
  ```typescript
  if (signal?.aborted) return;
  ```
- Callbacks are NOT invoked for the aborted stream
- The `setMessages()` call in `loadMessages()` replaces the incomplete streaming message with the loaded conversation

---

### 9. **Segment Preservation & Restoration**

When saving a conversation to the backend, the `ContentSegment[]` is serialized to `metadata` JSON.

When loading a conversation from `selectConversation()`:
```typescript
if (msg.metadata) {
  try {
    const meta = typeof msg.metadata === 'string' ? JSON.parse(msg.metadata) : msg.metadata;
    if (Array.isArray(meta?.segments)) {
      segments = (meta.segments as Array<Record<string, unknown>>).map((seg) => {
        // Reconstruct each segment with type-specific fields
        const s: ContentSegment = { type: seg.type as ContentSegment['type'] };
        if (seg.text) s.text = seg.text as string;
        if (seg.toolCallId || seg.toolName) {
          s.toolResult = { /* ... reconstruct ToolCallResult ... */ };
        }
        if (seg.chart_spec) s.chart_spec = seg.chart_spec;
        // ... more fields ...
        return s;
      });
    }
  } catch {
    // Fallback to single answer segment
  }
}
```

This allows charts, tool results, subagent outputs, and thinking blocks to be fully restored.

---

### 10. **Summary of Key Refs & State Variables**

**In AgentContext:**
- `messages` - The rendered message array (useState)
- `isStreaming` - Whether a response is in progress (useState)
- `abortRef` - Current AbortController for fetch cancellation
- `textBufferRef` - Accumulates text chunks for 50ms before flushing
- `flushTimerRef` - Timer ID for debounced text flush
- `assistantIdRef` - ID of the current streaming assistant message
- `segmentsRef` - Accumulates ContentSegments for current message
- `currentTextRef` - Current text being accumulated in thinking/answer phase
- `phaseRef` - Tracks 'thinking' vs 'answer' phase
- `sessionIdRef` - Session ID for resuming multi-turn conversations
- `pendingHistoryRef` - Message history to pass after a delete

**In ConversationContext:**
- `activeConversationId` - Currently selected conversation (or null for new)
- `refreshTrigger` - Counter to trigger sidebar refresh

### 11. **AgentPanel Component** 

Renders the UI based on messages array:

**Location:** `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/implement-memory/frontend/src/components/AgentPanel.tsx`

```typescript
export function AgentPanel({ tables, onUpload, onLoadSample, pendingSkillCommand, onSkillCommandConsumed }: AgentPanelProps) {
  const { t } = useTranslation();
  const { messages, clearMessages } = useAgent();
  const bottomRef = useRef<HTMLDivElement>(null);
  const scrollContainerRef = useRef<HTMLDivElement>(null);

  // Auto-scroll to bottom when messages change
  useEffect(() => {
    if (!userScrolledUp.current) {
      bottomRef.current?.scrollIntoView({ behavior: 'smooth' });
    }
  }, [messages]);

  return (
    <div className="agent-panel">
      <div className="agent-panel__header">
        {/* Actions: export, clear */}
      </div>
      <div className="agent-panel__messages">
        {messages.map((msg, index) => (
          <MessageBubble key={msg.id} message={msg} messageIndex={index} />
        ))}
      </div>
      <ChatInput />
    </div>
  );
}
```

- Maps `messages` array to `MessageBubble` components
- Each bubble renders a `ChatMessage` with its segments
- Auto-scrolls when messages change (unless user scrolled up)
- `MessageBubble` handles rendering of different segment types (thinking, answer, tool calls, charts, etc.)

---

### Key Design Patterns

1. **Ref-based Streaming**: Uses refs to accumulate data without triggering renders
2. **Debounced Flush**: 50ms debounce prevents excessive state updates during streaming
3. **Segment-based Rendering**: Rather than flat content, messages have structured segments for rich rendering
4. **Explicit Abort**: Uses AbortController to cancel in-flight requests when switching conversations
5. **Metadata Persistence**: Segments serialized to JSON metadata for full conversation restoration
6. **History Building**: Each message sends conversation history for multi-turn context
7. **Session Tracking**: Session IDs allow backend to maintain conversation state across messages

This architecture ensures smooth streaming, proper cleanup on conversation switches, and full restoration of rich message history with charts, tool calls, and thinking blocks.

   280→            abortRef.current = null;
   281→          },
   282→          onUserQuestion: (data) => {
   283→            if (flushTimerRef.current) {
   284→              clearTimeout(flushTimerRef.current);
   285→              flushTimerRef.current = null;
   286→            }
   287→            flushText();
   288→            if (currentTextRef.current.trim()) {

   295→              questionData: data,

   299→                m.id === assistantId

   313→
   314→  const editMessage = useCallback(
   315→    async (messageIndex: number, newContent: string) => {
   316→      if (isStreaming) return;
   317→
   318→      // Build conversation history from messages before the edit point
   319→      const conversationHistory: { role: string; content: string }[] = [];
   320→      for (let i = 0; i < messageIndex; i++) {
   321→        const msg = messages[i];
   322→        if (msg.role === 'user' || msg.role === 'assistant') {
   323→          conversationHistory.push({ role: msg.role, content: msg.content });
   324→        }
   325→      }
   326→
   327→      const assistantId = generateId();
   328→      assistantIdRef.current = assistantId;

   330→      const userMsg: ChatMessage = {

   336→        id: assistantId,
   337→        role: 'assistant',
   338→        content: '',
   339→        toolCalls: [],
   340→        isStreaming: true,
   341→      };
   342→
   343→      setMessages((prev) => [...prev.slice(0, messageIndex), userMsg, assistantMsg]);
   344→      setIsStreaming(true);

   350→      const controller = new AbortController();

   353→      await runAgentEditLoop(

   358→          onTextChunk: (chunk) => {
   359→            textBufferRef.current += chunk;

   362→                flushText();
   363→                flushTimerRef.current = null;
   364→              }, 50);
   365→            }
   366→          },
   367→          onThinkingDone: () => {
   368→            if (flushTimerRef.current) {

   372→            flushText();
   373→            if (currentTextRef.current.trim()) {

   382→                  : m
   383→              )
   384→            );
   385→          },
   386→          onToolCall: (pending: ToolCallResult) => {
   387→            if (flushTimerRef.current) {

   397→            segmentsRef.current.push({ type: 'tool', toolResult: pending });
   398→            setMessages((prev) =>
   399→              prev.map((m) =>
   400→                m.id === assistantId
   401→                  ? { ...m, currentPhase: 'answer', segments: [...segmentsRef.current] }
   402→                  : m
   403→              )
   404→            );
   405→          },
   406→          onToolResult: (result: ToolCallResult) => {
   407→            const pendingIdx = segmentsRef.current.findIndex(
   408→              (s) => s.type === 'tool' && s.toolResult?.toolCallId === result.toolCallId
   409→            );
   410→            if (pendingIdx !== -1) {
   411→              const pending = segmentsRef.current[pendingIdx].toolResult!;
   412→              segmentsRef.current[pendingIdx] = {
   413→                type: 'tool',

   415→                  ...pending,

   423→            } else {

   425→            }
   426→            setMessages((prev) =>
   427→              prev.map((m) =>
   428→                m.id === assistantId
   429→                  ? { ...m, toolCalls: [...(m.toolCalls || []), result], segments: [...segmentsRef.current] }
   430→                  : m

   435→          onSubagentStart: (data) => {

   469→            setMessages((prev) =>
   470→              prev.map((m) =>
   471→                m.id === assistantId

   475→            );
   476→          },
   477→          onDone: (newSessionId) => {

   479→            if (flushTimerRef.current) {

   481→              flushTimerRef.current = null;

   483→            flushText();
   484→            if (currentTextRef.current.trim()) {

   486→              currentTextRef.current = '';

   492→                  : m

   514→                  : m

   516→            );
   517→            setIsStreaming(false);
   518→            abortRef.current = null;
   519→          },
   520→          onUserQuestion: (data) => {
   521→            if (flushTimerRef.current) {

   523→              flushTimerRef.current = null;
   524→            }
   525→            flushText();
   526→            if (currentTextRef.current.trim()) {
   527→              const segType = phaseRef.current === 'answer' ? 'answer' : 'thinking';
   528→              segmentsRef.current.push({ type: segType, text: currentTextRef.current });
   529→              currentTextRef.current = '';
   530→            }
   531→            segmentsRef.current.push({
   532→              type: 'user_question',
   533→              questionData: data,
   534→            });
   535→            setMessages((prev) =>
   536→              prev.map((m) =>
   537→                m.id === assistantId
   538→                  ? { ...m, segments: [...segmentsRef.current] }
   539→                  : m
   540→              )
   541→            );
   542→          },
   543→        },
   544→        controller.signal,
   545→        userSessionId,
   546→      );
   547→    },
   548→    [isStreaming, messages, flushText, refreshTables, userSessionId]
   549→  );
   550→
   551→  const deleteMessage = useCallback(
   552→    (messageIndex: number) => {
   553→      if (isStreaming) return;
   554→
   555→      // Store remaining messages as history for Langfuse context on next send
   556→      setMessages((prev) => {
   557→        const remaining = prev.slice(0, messageIndex);
   558→        pendingHistoryRef.current = remaining
   559→          .filter((m) => m.role === 'user' || m.role === 'assistant')
   560→          .map((m) => ({ role: m.role, content: m.content }));
   561→        return remaining;
   562→      });
   563→
   564→      // Clear session so next message starts fresh
   565→      sessionIdRef.current = null;
   566→    },
   567→    [isStreaming]
   568→  );
   569→
   570→  const respondToQuestion = useCallback(
   571→    async (questionId: string, answers: string[], freeText?: string) => {
   572→      // Update the segment to show the user's answer
   573→      const segIdx = segmentsRef.current.findIndex(
   574→        (s) => s.type === 'user_question' && s.questionData?.questionId === questionId
   575→      );
   576→      if (segIdx !== -1) {
   577→        segmentsRef.current[segIdx] = {
   578→          ...segmentsRef.current[segIdx],
   579→          userAnswer: answers,
   580→          userFreeText: freeText,
   581→        };
   582→        setMessages((prev) =>
   583→          prev.map((m) =>
   584→            m.id === assistantIdRef.current
   585→              ? { ...m, segments: [...segmentsRef.current] }
   586→              : m
   587→          )
   588→        );
   589→      }
   590→
   591→      // POST to backend
   592→      try {
   593→        await fetch('/api/chat/respond', {
   594→          method: 'POST',
   595→          headers: {
   596→            'Content-Type': 'application/json',
   597→            ...(userSessionId ? { 'X-Session-ID': userSessionId } : {}),
   598→          },
   599→          body: JSON.stringify({
   600→            question_id: questionId,
   601→            answers,
   602→            free_text: freeText || null,
   603→          }),
   604→        });
   605→      } catch (e) {
   606→        console.error('Failed to respond to question:', e);
   607→      }
   608→    },
   609→    [userSessionId]
   610→  );
   611→
   612→  const loadMessages = useCallback((msgs: ChatMessage[]) => {
   613→    // Abort any ongoing stream before loading a different conversation
   614→    if (abortRef.current) {
   615→      abortRef.current.abort();
   616→      abortRef.current = null;
   617→    }
   618→    if (flushTimerRef.current) {
   619→      clearTimeout(flushTimerRef.current);
   620→      flushTimerRef.current = null;
   621→    }
   622→    setMessages(msgs);
   623→    setIsStreaming(false);
   624→    textBufferRef.current = '';
   625→    currentTextRef.current = '';
   626→    segmentsRef.current = [];
   627→    assistantIdRef.current = '';
   628→    phaseRef.current = 'thinking';
   629→    sessionIdRef.current = null;
   630→    pendingHistoryRef.current = null;
   631→  }, []);
   632→
   633→  const clearMessages = useCallback(() => {
   634→    if (abortRef.current) {
   635→      abortRef.current.abort();
   636→    }
   637→    setMessages([]);
   638→    setIsStreaming(false);
   639→    sessionIdRef.current = null;
   640→    pendingHistoryRef.current = null;
   641→  }, []);
   642→
   643→  return (
   644→    <AgentContext.Provider
   645→      value={{ messages, isStreaming, sendMessage, editMessage, deleteMessage, clearMessages, loadMessages, respondToQuestion }}
   646→    >
   647→      {children}
   648→    </AgentContext.Provider>
   649→  );
   650→}
   651→

     1→from fastapi import APIRouter, Depends, Header
     2→from fastapi.responses import JSONResponse, StreamingResponse
     3→from pydantic import BaseModel
     4→
     5→from app.agent import stream_chat
     6→from app.database import Database
     7→from app.dependencies import get_session_db
     8→from app.pending_questions import pending_question_store
     9→
    10→router = APIRouter(prefix="/api", tags=["chat"])
    11→
    12→
    13→class ChatRequest(BaseModel):
    14→    message: str
    15→    session_id: str | None = None
    16→    langfuse_session_id: str | None = None
    17→    conversation_history: list[dict] = []
    18→    skills: list[str] | None = None
    19→    conversation_id: str | None = None
    20→
    21→
    22→class ChatEditRequest(BaseModel):
    23→    new_message: str
    24→    conversation_history: list[dict] = []
    25→    langfuse_session_id: str | None = None
    26→
    27→
    28→class QuestionResponseRequest(BaseModel):
    29→    question_id: str
    30→    answers: list[str] = []
    31→    free_text: str | None = None
    32→
    33→
    34→@router.post("/chat")
    35→async def chat(
    36→    request: ChatRequest,
    37→    db: Database = Depends(get_session_db),
    38→    x_session_id: str = Header(...),
    39→):
    40→    return StreamingResponse(
    41→        stream_chat(
    42→            request.message,
    43→            request.session_id,
    44→            db,
    45→            conversation_history=request.conversation_history or None,
    46→            langfuse_session_id=request.langfuse_session_id,
    47→            backend_session_id=x_session_id,
    48→            skills=request.skills,
    49→            conversation_id=request.conversation_id,
    50→        ),
    51→        media_type="text/event-stream",
    52→        headers={
    53→            "Cache-Control": "no-cache",
    54→            "Connection": "keep-alive",
    55→            "X-Accel-Buffering": "no",
    56→        },
    57→    )
    58→
    59→
    60→@router.post("/chat/edit")
    61→async def chat_edit(
    62→    request: ChatEditRequest,
    63→    db: Database = Depends(get_session_db),
    64→    x_session_id: str = Header(...),
    65→):
    66→    """Edit a message: start a fresh session with conversation history as context."""
    67→    return StreamingResponse(
    68→        stream_chat(
    69→            request.new_message,
    70→            session_id=None,
    71→            db=db,
    72→            conversation_history=request.conversation_history,
    73→            langfuse_session_id=request.langfuse_session_id,
    74→            backend_session_id=x_session_id,
    75→        ),
    76→        media_type="text/event-stream",
    77→        headers={
    78→            "Cache-Control": "no-cache",
    79→            "Connection": "keep-alive",
    80→            "X-Accel-Buffering": "no",
    81→        },
    82→    )
    83→
    84→
    85→@router.post("/chat/respond")
    86→async def respond_to_question(
    87→    request: QuestionResponseRequest,
    88→    x_session_id: str = Header(...),
    89→):
    90→    """Respond to a pending user question from the agent."""
    91→    pending = pending_question_store.get_pending(x_session_id)
    92→    if pending is None or pending["question_id"] != request.question_id:
    93→        return JSONResponse(status_code=404, content={"error": "No pending question found"})
    94→
    95→    answer = {"answers": request.answers}
    96→    if request.free_text:
    97→        answer["free_text"] = request.free_text
    98→    pending_question_store.respond(x_session_id, request.question_id, answer)
    99→    return {"status": "ok"}
   100→

     1→import type { ToolCallResult, UserQuestionData, UserQuestionOption } from '../types';
     2→
     3→interface AgentCallbacks {
     4→  onTextChunk: (text: string) => void;
     5→  onThinkingDone: () => void;
     6→  onToolCall: (pending: ToolCallResult) => void;
     7→  onToolResult: (result: ToolCallResult) => void;
     8→  onSubagentStart?: (data: { id: string; name: string; prompt: string }) => void;
     9→  onSubagentEnd?: (data: { id: string; name: string; result?: string; sql_results?: Array<{ sql: string; columns?: string[]; rows?: Record<string, unknown>[]; rowCount?: number; error?: string }>; chart_spec?: { data: unknown[]; layout?: Record<string, unknown> } }) => void;
    10→  onDone: (sessionId: string | null) => void;
    11→  onError: (error: string) => void;
    12→  onUserQuestion?: (data: UserQuestionData) => void;
    13→}
    14→
    15→export type { AgentCallbacks };
    16→
    17→async function streamSSE(
    18→  response: Response,
    19→  callbacks: AgentCallbacks,
    20→  signal?: AbortSignal,
    21→): Promise<void> {
    22→  const reader = response.body?.getReader();
    23→  if (!reader) {
    24→    callbacks.onError('No response stream');
    25→    return;
    26→  }
    27→
    28→  let doneReceived = false;
    29→  const wrappedCallbacks: AgentCallbacks = {
    30→    ...callbacks,
    31→    onDone: (sessionId) => {
    32→      doneReceived = true;
    33→      callbacks.onDone(sessionId);
    34→    },
    35→    onError: (error) => {
    36→      doneReceived = true;
    37→      callbacks.onError(error);
    38→    },
    39→  };
    40→
    41→  try {
    42→    const decoder = new TextDecoder();
    43→    let buffer = '';
    44→    let eventType = '';
    45→
    46→    while (true) {
    47→      const { done, value } = await reader.read();
    48→      if (done) break;
    49→
    50→      buffer += decoder.decode(value, { stream: true });
    51→      const lines = buffer.split('\n');
    52→      buffer = lines.pop() ?? '';
    53→      for (const line of lines) {
    54→        if (line.startsWith('event: ')) {
    55→          eventType = line.slice(7).trim();
    56→        } else if (line.startsWith('data: ')) {
    57→          const data = line.slice(6);
    58→          try {
    59→            const parsed = JSON.parse(data);
    60→            handleSSEEvent(eventType, parsed, wrappedCallbacks);
    61→          } catch {
    62→            // Skip malformed JSON
    63→          }
    64→          eventType = '';
    65→        }
    66→      }
    67→    }
    68→
    69→    // Process any remaining data left in buffer after stream ends
    70→    if (buffer.trim()) {
    71→      const remainingLines = buffer.split('\n');
    72→      for (const line of remainingLines) {
    73→        if (line.startsWith('event: ')) {
    74→          eventType = line.slice(7).trim();
    75→        } else if (line.startsWith('data: ')) {
    76→          const data = line.slice(6);
    77→          try {
    78→            const parsed = JSON.parse(data);
    79→            handleSSEEvent(eventType, parsed, wrappedCallbacks);
    80→          } catch {
    81→            // Skip malformed JSON
    82→          }
    83→          eventType = '';
    84→        }
    85→      }
    86→    }
    87→
    88→    // Safety net: if stream ended without done/error, force done
    89→    if (!doneReceived) {
    90→      callbacks.onDone(null);
    91→    }
    92→  } catch (e: unknown) {
    93→    if (signal?.aborted) return;
    94→    const msg = e instanceof Error ? e.message : 'Connection failed';
    95→    callbacks.onError(msg);
    96→  }
    97→}
    98→
    99→export async function runAgentLoop(
   100→  message: string,
   101→  agentSessionId: string | null,
   102→  langfuseSessionId: string | null,
   103→  conversationHistory: { role: string; content: string }[] | null,
   104→  conversationId?: string | null,
   105→  callbacks?: AgentCallbacks,
   106→  signal?: AbortSignal,
   107→  userSessionId?: string,
   108→  skills?: string[],
   109→): Promise<void> {
   110→  if (!callbacks) return;
   111→  try {
   112→    const response = await fetch('/api/chat', {
   113→      method: 'POST',
   114→      headers: {
   115→        'Content-Type': 'application/json',
   116→        ...(userSessionId ? { 'X-Session-ID': userSessionId } : {}),
   117→      },
   118→      body: JSON.stringify({
   119→        message,
   120→        session_id: agentSessionId,
   121→        conversation_id: conversationId,
   122→        langfuse_session_id: langfuseSessionId,
   123→        conversation_history: conversationHistory ?? [],
   124→        ...(skills?.length ? { skills } : {}),
   125→      }),
   126→      signal,
   127→    });
   128→
   129→    if (!response.ok) {
   130→      const errorText = await response.text();
   131→      callbacks.onError(`Server error: ${errorText}`);
   132→      return;
   133→    }
   134→
   135→    await streamSSE(response, callbacks, signal);
   136→  } catch (e: unknown) {
   137→    if (signal?.aborted) return;
   138→    const msg = e instanceof Error ? e.message : 'Connection failed';
   139→    callbacks.onError(msg);
   140→  }
   141→}
   142→
   143→export async function runAgentEditLoop(
   144→  newMessage: string,
   145→  conversationHistory: { role: string; content: string }[],
   146→  langfuseSessionId: string | null,
   147→  callbacks: AgentCallbacks,
   148→  signal?: AbortSignal,
   149→  userSessionId?: string,
   150→): Promise<void> {
   151→  try {
   152→    const response = await fetch('/api/chat/edit', {
   153→      method: 'POST',
   154→      headers: {
   155→        'Content-Type': 'application/json',
   156→        ...(userSessionId ? { 'X-Session-ID': userSessionId } : {}),
   157→      },
   158→      body: JSON.stringify({
   159→        new_message: newMessage,
   160→        conversation_history: conversationHistory,
   161→        langfuse_session_id: langfuseSessionId,
   162→      }),
   163→      signal,
   164→    });
   165→
   166→    if (!response.ok) {
   167→      const errorText = await response.text();
   168→      callbacks.onError(`Server error: ${errorText}`);
   169→      return;
   170→    }
   171→
   172→    await streamSSE(response, callbacks, signal);
   173→  } catch (e: unknown) {
   174→    if (signal?.aborted) return;
   175→    const msg = e instanceof Error ? e.message : 'Connection failed';
   176→    callbacks.onError(msg);
   177→  }
   178→}
   179→
   180→function handleSSEEvent(
   181→  eventType: string,
   182→  data: Record<string, unknown>,
   183→  callbacks: AgentCallbacks,
   184→) {
   185→  switch (eventType) {
   186→    case 'thinking':
   187→    case 'answer':
   188→      callbacks.onTextChunk(data.text as string);
   189→      break;
   190→    case 'thinking_done':
   191→      callbacks.onThinkingDone();
   192→      break;
   193→    case 'tool_call':
   194→      callbacks.onToolCall({
   195→        toolCallId: (data.id as string) ?? '',
   196→        toolName: (data.name as string) ?? undefined,
   197→        sql: (data.sql as string) ?? '',
   198→        command: (data.command as string) ?? undefined,
   199→        toolInput: (data.input as Record<string, unknown>) ?? undefined,
   200→        columns: [],
   201→        rows: [],
   202→        rowCount: 0,
   203→      });
   204→      break;
   205→    case 'tool_result': {
   206→      const result: ToolCallResult = {
   207→        toolCallId: (data.id as string) ?? '',
   208→        toolName: (data.name as string) ?? undefined,
   209→        sql: (data.sql as string) ?? '',
   210→        columns: (data.columns as string[]) ?? [],
   211→        rows: (data.rows as Record<string, unknown>[]) ?? [],
   212→        rowCount: (data.rowCount as number) ?? 0,
   213→        error: (data.error as string) ?? undefined,
   214→        output: (data.output as string) ?? undefined,
   215→        rawContent: (data.content as string) ?? undefined,
   216→        chart_spec: (data.chart_spec as { data: unknown[]; layout?: Record<string, unknown> }) ?? undefined,
   217→      };
   218→      callbacks.onToolResult(result);
   219→      break;
   220→    }
   221→    case 'subagent_start':
   222→      callbacks.onSubagentStart?.({
   223→        id: data.id as string,
   224→        name: data.name as string,
   225→        prompt: (data.prompt as string) ?? '',
   226→      });
   227→      break;
   228→    case 'subagent_end': {
   229→      type ChartSpec = { data: unknown[]; layout?: Record<string, unknown>; frames?: unknown[] };
   230→      // Backend sends chart_specs (array) for multi-chart support.
   231→      // Fall back to legacy chart_spec (single object) for backwards compat.
   232→      const rawSpecs = data.chart_specs as ChartSpec[] | undefined;
   233→      const legacySpec = data.chart_spec as ChartSpec | undefined;
   234→      const chartSpecs: ChartSpec[] = rawSpecs ?? (legacySpec ? [legacySpec] : []);
   235→
   236→      if (chartSpecs.length > 0) {
   237→        // Emit one callback per chart so each gets its own segment
   238→        for (const spec of chartSpecs) {
   239→          callbacks.onSubagentEnd?.({
   240→            id: data.id as string,
   241→            name: data.name as string,
   242→            chart_spec: spec,
   243→          });
   244→        }
   245→        // Emit text result separately (if any) so it doesn't get lost
   246→        if (data.result) {
   247→          callbacks.onSubagentEnd?.({
   248→            id: data.id as string,
   249→            name: data.name as string,
   250→            result: data.result as string,
   251→          });
   252→        }
   253→      } else {
   254→        callbacks.onSubagentEnd?.({
   255→          id: data.id as string,
   256→          name: data.name as string,
   257→          result: (data.result as string) ?? undefined,
   258→          sql_results: (data.sql_results as Array<{ sql: string; columns?: string[]; rows?: Record<string, unknown>[]; rowCount?: number; error?: string }>) ?? undefined,
   259→        });
   260→      }
   261→      break;
   262→    }
   263→    case 'user_question': {
   264→      // Normalize options: the agent may send plain strings or {label, description} objects
   265→      const rawOptions = (data.options as unknown[]) ?? [];
   266→      const normalizedOptions: UserQuestionOption[] = (Array.isArray(rawOptions) ? rawOptions : []).map(
   267→        (opt: unknown) => {
   268→          if (typeof opt === 'string') return { label: opt };
   269→          if (typeof opt === 'object' && opt !== null && 'label' in opt) return opt as UserQuestionOption;
   270→          return { label: String(opt) };
   271→        }
   272→      );
   273→      callbacks.onUserQuestion?.({
   274→        questionId: data.question_id as string,
   275→        question: data.question as string,
   276→        options: normalizedOptions,
   277→        multiSelect: (data.multi_select as boolean) ?? false,
   278→      });
   279→      break;
   280→    }
   281→    case 'done':
   282→      callbacks.onDone((data.session_id as string) ?? null);
   283→      break;
   284→    case 'error':
   285→      callbacks.onError((data.message as string) ?? 'Unknown error');
   286→      break;
   287→  }
   288→}
   289→
```

> AGENT

Now I have a clear picture of the root cause.

## Root Cause Analysis

**Phase 1 complete.** The bug is:

1. User sends a message → backend immediately persists the **user message** (agent.py:198)
2. Streaming begins, assistant response builds up
3. User switches conversations → frontend calls `loadMessages()` which calls `abortRef.current.abort()` (AgentContext.tsx:614-616)
4. The frontend fetch is aborted → the HTTP connection closes
5. FastAPI/Starlette stops iterating the `stream_chat()` async generator
6. **The persistence code at agent.py:686-713 never executes** because it's inside the `try` block after the streaming loop — the generator is terminated before reaching it
7. When the user switches back → `selectConversation()` fetches from DB → only the user message exists

**The assistant message is never saved to the database when the stream is interrupted by a conversation switch.**

## Fix

Move the persistence code into a `finally` block so it runs regardless of how the generator exits (normal completion, client disconnect, or exception).

+        id: msg.id,
+        role: 'assistant' as const,
+        content: msg.content,
+        segments,
+      };
+    });
     return chatMessages;
   }, []);

> AGENT

The failing test is a pre-existing sandbox issue (can't write to `/tmp/duckdb-data-agent-test-uuid.duckdb`) — not related to my change. 19 of 20 tests pass.

## Summary

**Root cause:** The assistant message persistence code in `stream_chat()` was inside the `try` block, positioned after the streaming loop. When the frontend aborts the SSE connection (user switches conversations), the async generator is terminated before reaching the persistence code. Result: the assistant message is never saved to the database.

**Fix:** Moved the persistence code to a `finally` block (agent.py:695) so it executes regardless of how the generator exits:
- Normal completion (stream finishes) 
- Client disconnect (user switches conversations mid-stream) 
- Exception during streaming 

The persistence code is all synchronous (`memory_store.add_message()`), so it's safe to call from a `finally` block in an async generator — no yields involved.

Perfect! Now I have all the information. Let me create a comprehensive summary of the data flow.

## Full Data Flow: GET /api/conversations/{id}

Here's the complete journey from database to API response and how it connects to the frontend:

### Backend Flow

**1. API Endpoint** (`/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/implement-memory/backend/app/routes/conversations.py`, lines 29-34)
```python
@router.get("/{conversation_id}")
async def get_conversation(conversation_id: str):
    conv = memory_store.get_conversation(conversation_id)
    if conv is None:
        return JSONResponse(status_code=404, content={"error": "Conversation not found"})
    return conv
```

**2. Memory Store Implementation** (`/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/implement-memory/backend/app/memory_store.py`, lines 133-155)

The `get_conversation()` method performs a two-step query:

- **Step 1 (lines 137-140)**: Query conversation metadata from SQLite
  ```python
  row = conn.execute(
      "SELECT * FROM conversations WHERE id = ?",
      (conversation_id,),
  ).fetchone()
  ```

- **Step 2 (lines 144-151)**: Fetch all associated messages in order
  ```python
  msgs = conn.execute(
      """
      SELECT * FROM messages
      WHERE conversation_id = ?
      ORDER BY sort_order
      """,
      (conversation_id,),
  ).fetchall()
  ```

- **Step 3 (line 154)**: Attach messages array to conversation dict
  ```python
  conv["messages"] = [dict(m) for m in msgs]
  return conv
  ```

**Database Schema** (lines 50-73):
- `conversations` table: id, user_id, title, created_at, updated_at
- `messages` table: id, conversation_id (FK), role, content, metadata, created_at, sort_order
- Index on messages(conversation_id, sort_order) ensures ordered retrieval

### Frontend Flow

**1. App.tsx** (lines 56-59)
```typescript
const handleConversationSelect = useCallback(async (id: string) => {
  const messages = await conversation.selectConversation(id);
  loadMessages(messages);
}, [conversation, loadMessages]);
```

**2. ConversationContext.tsx** (lines 52-111) - `selectConversation()` method

This is the critical transformation layer:

- **Fetch** (lines 53-55): GET request to `/api/conversations/{id}`
  ```typescript
  const res = await fetch(`/api/conversations/${id}`);
  const conv: Conversation = await res.json();
  ```

- **Parse Messages** (lines 58-109): Transforms raw DB messages into `ChatMessage[]`
  - For user messages (line 60): Returns simple `{ id, role: 'user', content }`
  - For assistant messages (lines 63-101):
    - Tries to parse `metadata` JSON to reconstruct `ContentSegment[]` (lines 64-96)
    - `ContentSegment` includes: type, text, toolResult (with SQL results), subagent info, chart specs
    - Falls back to single "answer" segment if metadata parsing fails (lines 99-101)

- **Set Active State** (line 56): Updates `activeConversationId` in context
- **Return ChatMessages** (line 110): Returns structured chat message array to App.tsx

**3. Agent Context Loading** (App.tsx line 58)
```typescript
loadMessages(messages);
```
This feeds the loaded conversation messages into the agent context for display/replay.

### Key Data Structure Transformations

| Layer | Format |
|-------|--------|
| **SQLite** | `conversations { id, user_id, title, created_at, updated_at }` + `messages { id, conversation_id, role, content, metadata (JSON string), created_at, sort_order }` |
| **API Response** | `{ id, user_id, title, created_at, updated_at, messages: [...] }` where messages are raw DB rows |
| **React State** | `ChatMessage[]` with restored `ContentSegment[]` from metadata, full type safety |

### Critical Implementation Details

1. **Metadata Handling** (ConversationContext lines 65-96): Assistant messages store rich segments as stringified JSON in the `metadata` column. The context restores this structure on load, allowing full reconstruction of tool results, charts, and subagent outputs.

2. **Thread Safety** (MemoryStore lines 24-26): All DB operations acquire `threading.Lock()` for write operations, WAL mode for concurrent reads.

3. **Sort Order** (MemoryStore line 148): Messages always retrieved ordered by `sort_order` to maintain conversation chronology.

4. **No 404 in Frontend** (ConversationContext line 54): Frontend throws if response isn't OK, triggering error handling up the call stack.

My previous fix was wrong. The `finally` block approach might not execute in time (or at all) due to async generator cleanup timing. Let me go back to Phase 1 and rethink.

**Re-analyzing the problem:** When the user switches conversations, the frontend:
1. Aborts the SSE stream
2. Replaces messages with the new conversation's messages (from API)
3. When switching back, fetches from API — but the backend `finally` block may not have run yet

The real fix should be **on the frontend**: preserve the in-progress messages before replacing them.

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/implement-memory/frontend/src/contexts/AgentContext.tsx",
  "old_string": "  const loadMessages = useCallback((msgs: ChatMessage[]) => {\n    // Abort any ongoing stream before loading a different conversation\n    if (abortRef.current) {\n      abortRef.current.abort();\n      abortRef.current = null;\n    }\n    if (flushTimerRef.current) {\n      clearTimeout(flushTimerRef.current);\n      flushTimerRef.current = null;\n    }\n    setMessages(msgs);\n    setIsStreaming(false);\n    textBufferRef.current = '';\n    currentTextRef.current = '';\n    segmentsRef.current = [];\n    assistantIdRef.current = '';\n    phaseRef.current = 'thinking';\n    sessionIdRef.current = null;\n    pendingHistoryRef.current = null;\n  }, []);",
  "new_string": "  const loadMessages = useCallback((msgs: ChatMessage[], outgoingConversationId?: string | null, incomingConversationId?: string | null) => {\n    // Abort any ongoing stream before loading a different conversation\n    if (abortRef.current) {\n      abortRef.current.abort();\n      abortRef.current = null;\n    }\n    if (flushTimerRef.current) {\n      clearTimeout(flushTimerRef.current);\n      flushTimerRef.current = null;\n    }\n\n    // Check cache for incoming conversation — use cached messages if they\n    // have more data than what the API returned (backend persistence may\n    // not have completed yet when the user switches back quickly).\n    let finalMsgs = msgs;\n    if (incomingConversationId) {\n      const cached = messagesCacheRef.current.get(incomingConversationId);\n      if (cached && cached.length > msgs.length) {\n        finalMsgs = cached;\n      }\n      // Clear used cache entry so stale data doesn't persist forever\n      messagesCacheRef.current.delete(incomingConversationId);\n    }\n\n    // Save current messages to cache before replacing them.\n    // Uses functional setMessages to read the latest state without\n    // needing `messages` in the dependency array.\n    setMessages(prev => {\n      if (outgoingConversationId && prev.length > 0) {\n        // Flush any pending text buffer into the streaming assistant message\n        const pendingText = textBufferRef.current;\n        const aId = assistantIdRef.current;\n\n        // Build finalized segments including any unflushed text\n        let finalSegments = [...segmentsRef.current];\n        const pendingSegmentText = currentTextRef.current + pendingText;\n        if (pendingSegmentText.trim()) {\n          const segType = phaseRef.current === 'answer' ? 'answer' : 'thinking';\n          finalSegments.push({ type: segType as ContentSegment['type'], text: pendingSegmentText });\n        }\n\n        const cleaned = prev.map(m => {\n          if (m.id === aId && m.isStreaming) {\n            return {\n              ...m,\n              content: m.content + pendingText,\n              isStreaming: false,\n              currentPhase: undefined,\n              segments: finalSegments.length > 0 ? finalSegments : m.segments,\n            };\n          }\n          return m;\n        });\n        messagesCacheRef.current.set(outgoingConversationId, cleaned);\n      }\n      return finalMsgs;\n    });\n\n    setIsStreaming(false);\n    textBufferRef.current = '';\n    currentTextRef.current = '';\n    segmentsRef.current = [];\n    assistantIdRef.current = '';\n    phaseRef.current = 'thinking';\n    sessionIdRef.current = null;\n    pendingHistoryRef.current = null;\n  }, []);"
}

{

  "new_string": "  const clearMessages = useCallback((outgoingConversationId?: string | null) => {\n    if (abortRef.current) {\n      abortRef.current.abort();\n    }\n    setMessages(prev => {\n      if (outgoingConversationId && prev.length > 0) {\n        const cleaned = prev.map(m =>\n          m.isStreaming ? { ...m, isStreaming: false, currentPhase: undefined } : m\n        );\n        messagesCacheRef.current.set(outgoingConversationId, cleaned);\n      }\n      return [];\n    });\n    setIsStreaming(false);\n    sessionIdRef.current = null;\n    pendingHistoryRef.current = null;\n  }, []);"
}

+              const s: ContentSegment = { type: seg.type as ContentSegment['type'] };
+              if (seg.text) s.text = seg.text as string;

+                s.toolResult = {
+                  toolCallId: (seg.toolCallId as string) || '',
+                  toolName: seg.toolName as string,
+                  sql: (seg.sql as string) || '',
+                  columns: [],
+                  rows: [],
+                  rowCount: (seg.rowCount as number) || 0,
+                  error: seg.error as string | undefined,

+                };
+              }

+              if (seg.subagentName) s.subagentName = seg.subagentName as string;
+              if (seg.sqlResults) s.sqlResults = seg.sqlResults as ContentSegment['sqlResults'];
+              if (seg.chart_spec && seg.type === 'subagent_end') {
+                s.chart_spec = seg.chart_spec as ContentSegment['chart_spec'];
+              }
+              return s;
+            });
+          }
+        } catch {
+          // metadata parse failed, fall through to default
+        }
+      }
+

+      if (!segments) {

+

> AGENT

The changes look clean and correct. Let me summarize what the fix does:

## Fix Summary

**Root cause:** When the user switches conversations, `loadMessages()` replaces the React messages array with data from the API. But if the backend hasn't finished persisting the assistant message yet (the async generator cleanup is delayed), the API only returns the user message. The in-memory assistant response is lost.

**Fix (frontend-side message cache):**

1. **`AgentContext.tsx`** — Added `messagesCacheRef` (a `Map<conversationId, ChatMessage[]>`):
   - **On switch-away**: `loadMessages` saves the current messages (with any pending text flushed and streaming state finalized) to the cache, keyed by the outgoing conversation ID
   - **On switch-back**: Before loading API messages, checks if the cache has richer data (more messages). If so, uses the cached version instead
   - Cache entries are cleared after use to prevent stale data

2. **`App.tsx`** — Passes conversation IDs to `loadMessages` and `clearMessages`:
   - `handleConversationSelect`: passes `outgoingId` (current) and `id` (target)
   - `handleNewConversation`: passes `outgoingId` before clearing

3. **Backend `finally`** (previous fix) remains as belt-and-suspenders — the backend will eventually persist the message too, so subsequent page reloads will also have the data.

   233→    #    changes after the first response, which would orphan the container)

   236→    try:
   237→        # Send SSE keepalive immediately so the HTTP response starts and
   238→        # intermediate proxies (Vite, nginx) don't drop the idle connection
   239→        # before we've finished the blocking Docker container creation.
   240→        yield ": keepalive\n\n"
   241→

   243→        # Run it in a thread executor so the event loop stays responsive and
   244→        # can continue flushing keepalives to the client during startup.

   246→        loop = asyncio.get_event_loop()

The variables like `assistant_text_parts`, `all_text_parts`, etc. are defined at line 325 — inside the `try` block but after container setup code that can fail. If the container creation fails (line 247-258), execution jumps to `except`, then `finally`, where those variables don't exist.

   237→    assistant_text_parts: list[str] = []
   238→    all_text_parts: list[str] = []
   239→    assistant_metadata: dict = {"sql_queries": [], "chart_specs": []}
   240→    persisted_segments: list[dict] = []
   241→    seg_thinking_buf: list[str] = []
   242→    seg_answer_buf: list[str] = []
   243→    seg_phase = "thinking"
   244→    actual_session_id = session_id
   245→
   246→    try:
   247→        # Send SSE keepalive immediately so the HTTP response starts and
   248→        # intermediate proxies (Vite, nginx) don't drop the idle connection
   249→        # before we've finished the blocking Docker container creation.
   250→        yield ": keepalive\n\n"
   251→
   252→        # container_manager.create() is synchronous (blocking Docker API call).
```

> AGENT

The `UnboundLocalError` is fixed. The persistence variables are now declared before the `try` block so they're always accessible in the `finally` block, even if the `try` fails early (e.g., container creation error).

    12→/**
    13→ * Strip chart_spec JSON code blocks from answer text.
    14→ * The orchestrator may include the raw chart spec JSON even though the chart
    15→ * is rendered separately. Remove ```json ... ``` blocks containing chart_spec
    16→ * or Plotly trace data so they don't appear as raw text.
    17→ */
    18→function stripChartSpecBlocks(text: string): string {
    19→  return text.replace(/```(?:json)?\s*\n?\s*\{[\s\S]*?"(?:chart_spec|data)"[\s\S]*?\}\s*\n?\s*```/g, '').trim();
    20→}
    21→
    22→/**
    23→ * Fix missing line breaks before bold section headers in text.
    24→ * The model sometimes concatenates bold headers directly after the previous
    25→ * sentence (e.g. "...for arrays.**Preparing data arrays**"). This inserts
    26→ * paragraph breaks so they render on new lines.
    27→ */
    28→function fixBoldHeaderLineBreaks(text: string): string {
    29→  // Insert \n\n before **Header** when preceded by sentence-ending punctuation
    30→  // with no whitespace. The [A-Z] ensures we only match section-header-style
    31→  // bold (starting with a capital letter), avoiding inline bold like **data**.
    32→  return text.replace(/([.!?:])(\*\*[A-Z])/g, '$1\n\n$2');
    33→}
    34→
    35→/** Strip markdown formatting for plain-text preview display. */
    36→function stripMarkdown(text: string): string {
    37→  return text
    38→    .replace(/\*\*(.+?)\*\*/g, '$1')   // **bold**
    39→    .replace(/__(.+?)__/g, '$1')        // __bold__
    40→    .replace(/\*(.+?)\*/g, '$1')        // *italic*
    41→    .replace(/_(.+?)_/g, '$1')          // _italic_
    42→    .replace(/`(.+?)`/g, '$1')          // `code`
    43→    .replace(/^#+\s+/gm, '');           // # headings
    44→}

> AGENT

Now I see the issue. When a cached message has only thinking segments (no answer), the `ThinkingBlock` renders as a collapsed `<details>` element. The answer section is empty. Result: empty bubble.

Line 88: `open={isThinkingPhase || undefined}` — `isThinkingPhase` is `false` for non-streaming messages, so thinking is collapsed.
Lines 246-267: `answerBlockSegments` is empty since there are no answer segments.

The fix: when a non-streaming message has thinking but no answer, show the thinking block as open.

Clean. The fix: when a completed (non-streaming) message has thinking content but no answer, the thinking `<details>` defaults to **open** so the user can see the agent's in-progress thinking. When there IS an answer, thinking collapses as before.

     1→import json
     2→import logging
     3→
     4→from fastapi import APIRouter, Request
     5→from fastapi.responses import Response, StreamingResponse
     6→import httpx
     7→
     8→from app.config import MODEL_REWRITES, DEFAULT_TOOL_MODEL, BIFROST_BASE_URL
     9→
    10→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    11→
    12→ANTHROPIC_UPSTREAM = BIFROST_BASE_URL + "/anthropic"
    13→
    14→_SKIP_REQUEST_HEADERS = {
    15→    "host", "content-length", "transfer-encoding", "connection",
    16→}
    17→_SKIP_RESPONSE_HEADERS = {"transfer-encoding", "content-encoding", "connection"}
    18→
    19→router = APIRouter(prefix="/anthropic")
    20→
    21→
    22→def rewrite_model_in_body(
    23→    body: bytes, rewrites: dict[str, str], fallback: str = ""
    24→) -> bytes:
    25→    """Rewrite the 'model' field in a JSON body using the rewrites map.
    26→
    27→    Matches if the model string equals or contains a rewrite key
    28→    (e.g. 'sonnet' matches 'claude-sonnet-4-6').
    29→    If no rewrite matches and a fallback is set, rewrites to the fallback.
    30→    Returns the body unchanged if no match/fallback or if body is not valid JSON.
    31→    """
    32→    if not rewrites and not fallback:
    33→        return body
    34→    try:
    35→        data = json.loads(body)
    36→    except (json.JSONDecodeError, UnicodeDecodeError):
    37→        return body
    38→    model = data.get("model")
    39→    if not model or not isinstance(model, str):
    40→        return body
    41→    for tier, real_model in rewrites.items():
    42→        if model == tier or tier in model:
    43→            data["model"] = real_model
    44→            return json.dumps(data).encode()
    45→    if fallback:
    46→        data["model"] = fallback
    47→        return json.dumps(data).encode()
    48→    return body
    49→
    50→
    51→@router.api_route("/{path:path}", methods=["GET", "POST", "PUT", "DELETE", "PATCH"])
    52→async def proxy_to_upstream(path: str, request: Request):
    53→    headers = {
    54→        k: v for k, v in request.headers.items()
    55→        if k.lower() not in _SKIP_REQUEST_HEADERS
    56→    }
    57→
    58→    body = await request.body()
    59→
    60→    # Rewrite model on POST requests (messages, completions, etc.)
    61→    if request.method == "POST" and (MODEL_REWRITES or DEFAULT_TOOL_MODEL):
    62→        body = rewrite_model_in_body(body, MODEL_REWRITES, fallback=DEFAULT_TOOL_MODEL)
    63→
    64→    client = httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=httpx.Timeout(300.0))
    65→    try:
    66→        upstream_req = client.build_request(
    67→            method=request.method,
    68→            url=f"{ANTHROPIC_UPSTREAM}/{path}",
    69→            headers=headers,
    70→            content=body,
    71→        )
    72→        upstream_resp = await client.send(upstream_req, stream=True)
    73→
    74→        response_headers = {
    75→            k: v for k, v in upstream_resp.headers.items()
    76→            if k.lower() not in _SKIP_RESPONSE_HEADERS
    77→        }
    78→
    79→        # For error responses, read the full body and log it so we can
    80→        # debug upstream failures (e.g. Anthropic API 400 errors).
    81→        if upstream_resp.status_code >= 400:
    82→            error_body = await upstream_resp.aread()
    83→            await upstream_resp.aclose()
    84→            await client.aclose()
    85→            # Extract model from request for context
    86→            req_model = ""
    87→            try:
    88→                req_data = json.loads(body)
    89→                req_model = req_data.get("model", "")
    90→            except Exception:
    91→                pass
    92→            print(
    93→                f"[proxy] {upstream_resp.status_code} {request.method} /{path}"
    94→                f" model={req_model}:"
    95→                f" {error_body.decode('utf-8', errors='replace')[:2000]}",
    96→                flush=True,
    97→            )
    98→            return Response(
    99→                content=error_body,
   100→                status_code=upstream_resp.status_code,
   101→                headers=response_headers,
   102→            )
   103→
   104→        async def body_generator():
   105→            try:
   106→                async for chunk in upstream_resp.aiter_bytes():
   107→                    yield chunk
   108→            finally:
   109→                await upstream_resp.aclose()
   110→                await client.aclose()
   111→
   112→        return StreamingResponse(
   113→            body_generator(),
   114→            status_code=upstream_resp.status_code,
   115→            headers=response_headers,
   116→        )
   117→    except Exception:
   118→        await client.aclose()
   119→        logger.exception("Proxy request to upstream failed: %s %s", request.method, path)
   120→        raise
   121→
